### CaReAQA: A Cardiac and Respiratory Audio Question Answering Model for Open-Ended Diagnostic Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01199v1)
- **Authors**: Tsai-Ning Wang, Lin-Lin Chen, Neil Zeghidour, Aaqib Saeed
- **Abstract**: Medical audio signals, such as heart and lung sounds, play a crucial role in clinical diagnosis. However, analyzing these signals remains challenging: traditional methods rely on handcrafted features or supervised deep learning models that demand extensive labeled datasets, limiting their scalability and applicability. To address these issues, we propose CaReAQA, an audio-language model that integrates a foundation audio model with the reasoning capabilities of large language models, enabling clinically relevant, open-ended diagnostic responses. Alongside CaReAQA, we introduce CaReSound, a benchmark dataset of annotated medical audio recordings enriched with metadata and paired question-answer examples, intended to drive progress in diagnostic reasoning research. Evaluation results show that CaReAQA achieves 86.2% accuracy on open-ended diagnostic reasoning tasks, outperforming baseline models. It also generalizes well to closed-ended classification tasks, achieving an average accuracy of 56.9% on unseen datasets. Our findings show how audio-language integration and reasoning advances medical diagnostics, enabling efficient AI systems for clinical decision support.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CaReAQA, a novel audio-language model designed for open-ended diagnostic reasoning using cardiac and respiratory audio data. The model integrates a self-supervised audio foundation model with the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs).  Alongside CaReAQA, the authors present CaReSound, a new benchmark dataset of annotated medical audio recordings enriched with metadata and paired question-answer examples. The results show that CaReAQA outperforms baseline models in open-ended diagnostic reasoning and generalizes well to closed-ended classification tasks. The authors argue that their work advances medical diagnostics by enabling more efficient AI systems for clinical decision support.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The combination of a self-supervised audio foundation model with an LLM for *open-ended question answering* in the *specific domain* of cardiac and respiratory audio diagnostics provides a level of novelty. While audio-language models exist, the focus on medical audio and the open-ended diagnostic task, rather than simpler classification, distinguishes this work. The construction of CaReSound is also a significant contribution, given the scarcity of annotated datasets for this task.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the accuracy and accessibility of auscultation-based diagnosis. Current methods often rely on experienced clinicians or are limited to supervised learning with large labeled datasets.  CaReAQA aims to bridge this gap by providing a system capable of more nuanced and context-aware diagnostic support. It has the potential to be developed into clinical decision support tool, ultimately improving patient outcome.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Performance:** The model achieves compelling results, outperforming various baselines, in open-ended question answering and demonstrating good generalization to unseen data.
    *   **Benchmark Dataset:** The CaReSound dataset is a valuable resource for the research community, addressing a key bottleneck in medical audio AI.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper provides a well-defined methodology for model training, evaluation, and data generation.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies offer meaningful insights into the contributions of different components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The current system focuses only on cardiac and respiratory sounds. Expanding the model to incorporate more types of medical audio signals and related clinical data could further enhance its usefulness.
    *   **GPT-4 reliance:**. Reliance on a closed-source model (GPT-4) for evaluation adds a reproducibility barrier. The evaluation metrics in and of themselves don't really measure *diagnostic accuracy* so, this heavy reliance on GPT-4 could be considered a significant weakness from a clinical utility perspective.
    *   **Overstated Claims:** While the results are promising, the claim of "enabling efficient AI systems for clinical decision support" is a bit premature.  The model is still in the research stage and requires further validation and regulatory approvals before it can be used in real-world clinical settings.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** The ethical aspects of AI-assisted diagnosis, particularly in terms of potential bias, interpretability, and the role of human clinicians, should be carefully addressed to ensure that such systems are used responsibly.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    While the paper shows promise and demonstrates good results, its novelty and significance are incremental rather than revolutionary. The integration of existing techniques (self-supervised learning and LLMs) into a specific domain, although well-executed, doesn't represent a radical departure from existing approaches. The reliance on GPT-4 for a crucial part of evaluation reduces the reproducibilty of the evaluation process. Moreover, the clinical utility claim is premature without further real-world validation. Given this assessment, I would assign a score of **Score: 6.5**.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enabling Training-Free Semantic Communication Systems with Generative Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01209v1)
- **Authors**: Shunpu Tang, Yuanyuan Jia, Qianqian Yang, Ruichen Zhang, Jihong Park, Dusit Niyato
- **Abstract**: Semantic communication (SemCom) has recently emerged as a promising paradigm for next-generation wireless systems. Empowered by advanced artificial intelligence (AI) technologies, SemCom has achieved significant improvements in transmission quality and efficiency. However, existing SemCom systems either rely on training over large datasets and specific channel conditions or suffer from performance degradation under channel noise when operating in a training-free manner. To address these issues, we explore the use of generative diffusion models (GDMs) as training-free SemCom systems. Specifically, we design a semantic encoding and decoding method based on the inversion and sampling process of the denoising diffusion implicit model (DDIM), which introduces a two-stage forward diffusion process, split between the transmitter and receiver to enhance robustness against channel noise. Moreover, we optimize sampling steps to compensate for the increased noise level caused by channel noise. We also conduct a brief analysis to provide insights about this design. Simulations on the Kodak dataset validate that the proposed system outperforms the existing baseline SemCom systems across various metrics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a training-free semantic communication (SemCom) system for image transmission using generative diffusion models (GDMs). The core idea is to leverage pre-trained GDMs to encode and decode semantic information without requiring task-specific training.  The system utilizes the denoising diffusion implicit model (DDIM) and splits the forward diffusion process between the transmitter and receiver to improve robustness against channel noise.  The receiver optimizes the number of sampling steps to compensate for increased noise levels.  Simulations on the Kodak dataset demonstrate that the proposed system outperforms existing SemCom approaches, particularly in scenarios with low signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs). A key element is the use of DDIM inversion and a predefined text prompt to guide image reconstruction. The paper also provides an analysis of how channel noise shifts the latent distribution and how the proposed approach mitigates this mismatch.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-Free Approach:** Eliminating the need for extensive training is a significant advantage, making the system more adaptable and practical for real-world deployment.  The reliance on pre-trained models contributes to this flexibility.
    * **Noise Robustness:** Splitting the diffusion process and optimizing sampling steps demonstrate a well-considered approach to addressing channel noise, which is a crucial factor in wireless communication. The analysis provides theoretical justification for the design choices.
    * **Performance Improvement:**  The experimental results show substantial performance gains compared to established baselines, especially at low SNRs, which is a challenging scenario for traditional communication systems. The quantitative evaluations are comprehensive, utilizing various distortion, perceptual, and distribution metrics.
    * **Clear and Structured Presentation:**  The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the proposed method and its underlying principles. The inclusion of Algorithm 1 and the analysis in Proposition 1 enhances understanding.
    * **Addresses a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles the problem of efficiently and robustly transmitting images over noisy channels, a problem that is becoming increasingly important in many applications.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on a Fixed Text Prompt:** The use of a predefined text prompt ("High quality, High res, 8K") is somewhat limiting. While it avoids the need to transmit image captions, it restricts the system's ability to incorporate specific semantic information related to the image content. The prompt is likely optimized for generic images, and performance could suffer with images outside that domain. The system should ideally adapt the text prompt based on the content of the image itself.
    * **Limited Novelty in GDM Application:** While applying GDMs to SemCom is innovative, the specific techniques used for encoding/decoding (DDIM inversion and sampling) are relatively standard GDM operations. The novelty is more in the *application* of these techniques to the specific challenges of semantic communication than in developing fundamentally new GDM algorithms.
    * **Complexity of Implementation:** While training-free, the system still relies on a large pre-trained model (stable diffusion), which can be computationally expensive. The benefits in terms of bandwidth efficiency need to be weighed against the computational burden, especially in resource-constrained devices. The paper lacks a discussion of the computational complexity of the proposed approach.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While there is an ablation study of different TF,1 TF,2 and TB, more ablation studies would be good. It would be great to see more details of the individual contributions of different things to show that different parameters can be optimized.
    * **Lack of Direct Comparison to Other Training-Free Methods:** While the paper compares against WITT and SD-SemCom, a more detailed comparison, especially dissecting the reasons for the performance difference, would be useful.

* **Significance:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of semantic communication by demonstrating the feasibility of a training-free image transmission system using pre-trained GDMs. The approach offers a compelling alternative to traditional methods and deep learning-based systems that require extensive training. The split diffusion strategy and noise adaptation techniques are particularly relevant for noisy wireless environments. The paper could stimulate further research into the application of GDMs to other communication scenarios and the development of more sophisticated semantic encoding and decoding techniques. The ease of adaptation due to the lack of training makes it of great practical use.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I believe a score of **7** is appropriate. While the paper demonstrates a solid contribution to the field by presenting a practical, training-free SemCom system, the limited novelty in the GDM application and reliance on a fixed text prompt hold it back from achieving a higher score. The improvement compared to the baseline is significant, making it worth of future study.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IT
- **Score**: 7/10

### 2DXformer: Dual Transformers for Wind Power Forecasting with Dual Exogenous Variables
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01286v1)
- **Authors**: Yajuan Zhang, Jiahai Jiang, Yule Yan, Liang Yang, Ping Zhang
- **Abstract**: Accurate wind power forecasting can help formulate scientific dispatch plans, which is of great significance for maintaining the safety, stability, and efficient operation of the power system. In recent years, wind power forecasting methods based on deep learning have focused on extracting the spatiotemporal correlations among data, achieving significant improvements in forecasting accuracy. However, they exhibit two limitations. First, there is a lack of modeling for the inter-variable relationships, which limits the accuracy of the forecasts. Second, by treating endogenous and exogenous variables equally, it leads to unnecessary interactions between the endogenous and exogenous variables, increasing the complexity of the model. In this paper, we propose the 2DXformer, which, building upon the previous work's focus on spatiotemporal correlations, addresses the aforementioned two limitations. Specifically, we classify the inputs of the model into three types: exogenous static variables, exogenous dynamic variables, and endogenous variables. First, we embed these variables as variable tokens in a channel-independent manner. Then, we use the attention mechanism to capture the correlations among exogenous variables. Finally, we employ a multi-layer perceptron with residual connections to model the impact of exogenous variables on endogenous variables. Experimental results on two real-world large-scale datasets indicate that our proposed 2DXformer can further improve the performance of wind power forecasting. The code is available in this repository: \href{https://github.com/jseaj/2DXformer}{https://github.com/jseaj/2DXformer}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces 2DXformer, a novel transformer-based architecture for wind power forecasting. 2DXformer addresses limitations in existing deep learning methods by explicitly modeling inter-variable relationships and differentiating between endogenous (wind power) and exogenous (wind speed, temperature) variables.  The core innovation lies in its dual approach: it classifies exogenous variables into static and dynamic types, embeds them channel-independently, utilizes two separate Transformer blocks (EnTBlock for endogenous, ExTBlock for exogenous variables), and uses a ResidualMLP to model the influence of exogenous variables on the endogenous ones. Experiments on two real-world datasets (SDWPF and HHL16) demonstrate improved performance compared to several baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a modest degree of novelty. While spatio-temporal wind power forecasting using deep learning is an established area, 2DXformer introduces a specific architectural design based on dual exogenous variable treatment. The separation of exogenous variables into static and dynamic and the separate transformer blocks for endogenous and exogenous data are incremental improvements. The use of ResidualMLP to relate the variables is not particularly novel. The concept of classifying inputs and handling them differently (inspired by [9], [17]) provides *some* novelty, but it's more of an application of existing ideas to this specific domain.

*   **Significance:** The reported performance improvements on two datasets suggest that the proposed architecture has practical value. The code being publicly available is a positive aspect, enabling reproducibility and further research. Accurately forecasting wind power has undeniable real-world implications for grid management and renewable energy integration. However, the performance gains (reported in Table 1) are not so overwhelmingly significant to constitute a ground-breaking contribution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies limitations of existing wind power forecasting models: lack of inter-variable relationship modeling and indiscriminate treatment of endogenous/exogenous variables.
    *   **Well-Structured Architecture:**  The 2DXformer architecture is clearly explained, with detailed descriptions of each component (variable embeddings, EnTBlock, ExTBlock).
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The experimental setup includes multiple baseline methods and ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed components.
    *   **Real-World Datasets:**  The use of two real-world datasets enhances the practical relevance of the findings.
    *   **Code Availability**: The authors provide the code for their model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The performance gains are not dramatically larger than existing ST-B models like STAEformer, which questions the significance of the architectural changes.
    *   **Limited Innovation on the Individual Components:** The individual components (Transformers, ResidualMLP) are well-established. The novelty stems primarily from their combination and specific application to this problem.
    *   **Lack of Deeper Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of *why* 2DXformer outperforms other models. For example, visualization of attention weights to understand how the model captures inter-variable relationships would be beneficial.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The hyperparameter studies are rudimentary. A more thorough analysis of the impact of different hyperparameter settings on model performance across datasets would be helpful.
    *   **Justification of Choices:** The choice of ResidualMLP over other more sophisticated techniques for relating endogenous and exogenous variables needs stronger justification (beyond simply stating that ablation studies validated the choice).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper might influence researchers working on wind power forecasting or related time-series prediction problems. The specific architectural design and dual exogenous variable treatment could inspire further research in this area. However, the incremental nature of the contribution limits its potential for widespread impact.

*   **Overall:** This is a solid engineering paper demonstrating an improvement over existing methods by carefully designing an architecture tailored to wind power forecasting. However, the level of innovation is not high enough to justify a significantly higher score.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:** The 2DXformer provides a useful architectural modification for wind power forecasting. The separation and specialized handling of exogenous variables are likely useful in this domain. While the paper is well-executed, the improvements are incremental and the core components are not fundamentally new, preventing a higher rating. The model provides a clear performance benefit, but its potential to reshape the field is limited.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enhancing SPARQL Query Rewriting for Complex Ontology Alignments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01309v1)
- **Authors**: Anicet Lepetit Ondo, Laurence Capus, Mamadou Bousso
- **Abstract**: SPARQL query rewriting is a fundamental mechanism for uniformly querying heterogeneous ontologies in the Linked Data Web. However, the complexity of ontology alignments, particularly rich correspondences (c : c), makes this process challenging. Existing approaches primarily focus on simple (s : s) and partially complex ( s : c) alignments, thereby overlooking the challenges posed by more expressive alignments. Moreover, the intricate syntax of SPARQL presents a barrier for non-expert users seeking to fully exploit the knowledge encapsulated in ontologies. This article proposes an innovative approach for the automatic rewriting of SPARQL queries from a source ontology to a target ontology, based on a user's need expressed in natural language. It leverages the principles of equivalence transitivity as well as the advanced capabilities of large language models such as GPT-4. By integrating these elements, this approach stands out for its ability to efficiently handle complex alignments, particularly (c : c) correspondences , by fully exploiting their expressiveness. Additionally, it facilitates access to aligned ontologies for users unfamiliar with SPARQL, providing a flexible solution for querying heterogeneous data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of querying heterogeneous ontologies in the Linked Data Web, focusing on the challenges posed by complex ontology alignments, specifically those involving complex-to-complex (c:c) correspondences. It proposes a novel approach that integrates natural language querying with SPARQL query rewriting, leveraging large language models (LLMs) like GPT-4 and principles of equivalence transitivity. The aim is to enable users, regardless of their SPARQL expertise, to efficiently query aligned ontologies and exploit the full expressiveness of complex alignments. The approach involves translating natural language questions into subgraphs, identifying correspondences, applying equivalence transitivity using SWRL, and generating SPARQL queries for the target ontology. The paper also discusses experimental results obtained on the Conference dataset, showing the feasibility of the approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a worthwhile combination of several existing concepts, i.e., using natural language querying, SPARQL rewriting, and LLMs to address the problem of querying aligned ontologies. The emphasis on handling complex (c:c) alignments and leveraging equivalence transitivity is a distinguishing factor compared to prior work, which often focuses on simpler alignments. The idea of using GPT-4 to bridge the gap between natural language and SPARQL queries is also novel.

*   **Significance:** The ability to effectively query heterogeneous ontologies is crucial for data integration and interoperability in the Semantic Web. Overcoming the accessibility barrier for non-expert users and handling complex alignments contributes to better knowledge utilization. The research could have potential impact by enabling broader adoption of Semantic Web technologies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of querying aligned ontologies, particularly with complex alignments.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The proposed method covers a wide range of aspects, including natural language querying, correspondence identification, equivalence transitivity, and SPARQL generation.
    *   **Use of LLMs:**  The integration of LLMs provides an intuitive interface for non-expert users to interact with ontologies.
    *   **Evaluation:** The experimental results, even with a manually enriched dataset, demonstrate the feasibility and effectiveness of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The experimental evaluation is limited in scope. While it shows potential, the paper lacks extensive quantitative results or comparison with other query rewriting approaches. The manual enrichment of the dataset raises concerns about scalability and generalizability. A larger, publicly available dataset would improve credibility.

    *   **Lack of Detailed Implementation Information:** The paper describes the general architecture but lacks some crucial details regarding the precise prompts used with GPT-4 and the specifics of the subgraph generation process. The explanation of how existing restrictions in ontologies (cardinalities, universal and existential quantifiers) is accounted for isn't very specific, creating some questions.
    *   **Reliance on Equivalence Relations:** The paper acknowledges that the current implementation primarily relies on equivalence relations. Expanding the approach to handle subsumption relations is identified as future work.
    *   **Limited Novelty of individual components:** While the overall concept is worthwhile, the underlying components (natural language interfaces, SPARQL rewriting techniques, LLM usage) are independently well-established, which somewhat limits the originality. The paper focuses on the integration, and a detailed comparison to existing approaches would emphasize the added value.

*   **Potential Impact:** If further validated with larger datasets and compared to existing query rewriting algorithms, the proposed method could contribute significantly to the Semantic Web field by simplifying access to heterogeneous knowledge for a broader range of users.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the above analysis, I would assign a score of **6**.

*Rationale:* The paper addresses a relevant and important problem with a thoughtful and novel integration of existing techniques. The focus on complex alignments and the use of LLMs are promising. However, the limited empirical validation and reliance on equivalence relations prevent it from being considered a truly groundbreaking contribution. The lack of comparison to baseline methods and limited quantitative data further limits its claim to superiority. The paper shows potential, but it needs further validation and refinement to demonstrate its substantial impact within the field. The integration is interesting, but individually all the techniques are well established, limiting the novelty.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 6/10

### Helping Big Language Models Protect Themselves: An Enhanced Filtering and Summarization System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01315v1)
- **Authors**: Sheikh Samit Muhaimin, Spyridon Mastorakis
- **Abstract**: The recent growth in the use of Large Language Models has made them vulnerable to sophisticated adversarial assaults, manipulative prompts, and encoded malicious inputs. Existing countermeasures frequently necessitate retraining models, which is computationally costly and impracticable for deployment. Without the need for retraining or fine-tuning, this study presents a unique defense paradigm that allows LLMs to recognize, filter, and defend against adversarial or malicious inputs on their own. There are two main parts to the suggested framework: (1) A prompt filtering module that uses sophisticated Natural Language Processing (NLP) techniques, including zero-shot classification, keyword analysis, and encoded content detection (e.g. base64, hexadecimal, URL encoding), to detect, decode, and classify harmful inputs; and (2) A summarization module that processes and summarizes adversarial research literature to give the LLM context-aware defense knowledge. This approach strengthens LLMs' resistance to adversarial exploitation by fusing text extraction, summarization, and harmful prompt analysis. According to experimental results, this integrated technique has a 98.71% success rate in identifying harmful patterns, manipulative language structures, and encoded prompts. By employing a modest amount of adversarial research literature as context, the methodology also allows the model to react correctly to harmful inputs with a larger percentage of jailbreak resistance and refusal rate. While maintaining the quality of LLM responses, the framework dramatically increases LLM's resistance to hostile misuse, demonstrating its efficacy as a quick and easy substitute for time-consuming, retraining-based defenses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel two-stage framework to enhance the security and trustworthiness of Large Language Models (LLMs) against adversarial attacks. The framework consists of: 1) a prompt filtering module employing NLP techniques (zero-shot classification, keyword analysis, encoded content detection) to identify and classify harmful inputs, and 2) a summarization module that processes adversarial research literature to provide LLMs with context-aware defense knowledge. The framework aims to improve LLM resistance to adversarial exploitation without the need for retraining or fine-tuning. The authors demonstrate a high success rate (98.71%) in identifying harmful patterns and show improvements in jailbreak resistance and refusal rates while maintaining response quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of techniques rather than the individual methods themselves. Using prompt filtering and summarization is not new, but integrating them *specifically* for real-time, retraining-free adversarial defense of LLMs is a significant contribution. The adaptation of summarization to continuously inject adversarial research findings into the model's context is particularly innovative. This differs from static approaches that require periodic retraining. While techniques like zero-shot classification and keyword analysis are established NLP methods, their strategic application in a multi-layered prompt analysis pipeline adds value.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is substantial. The computational cost and impracticality of constant retraining make it challenging for researchers and experts to scale LLM defenses. The ability to defend against adversarial inputs "on the fly" is highly appealing and addresses a critical bottleneck. The paper's focus on *runtime* defense mechanisms is particularly relevant considering the rapid evolution of adversarial attacks. Furthermore, the integration of knowledge acquisition through summarization allows the model to adapt dynamically to new threats, which is crucial in real-world scenarios. The demonstrated improvement in jailbreak resistance and refusal rates supports the effectiveness of the approach. The emphasis on maintaining response quality (helpfulness) is also vital, as it ensures that security enhancements do not negatively impact usability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is lightweight and doesn't require retraining.
    *   It adapts dynamically to new adversarial research.
    *   It achieves a high malicious prompt detection rate.
    *   It improves jailbreak resistance and refusal rates.
    *   It maintains response helpfulness.
    *   The paper provides experimental results to support the claims and justifies design choices (e.g. bert-base-cnn summarization).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies on the quality and availability of adversarial research literature for the summarization module. If the literature is biased or incomplete, the defense may be ineffective.
    *   The reliance on keyword analysis might be susceptible to sophisticated attacks that use synonyms or semantically similar terms to bypass filtering.
    *   The prompt complexity analysis and zero-shot classification steps, while helpful, may add some computational overhead, and their effectiveness could vary depending on the specific LLM architecture.
    *   The dataset used for flagged filter-word generation might not represent all malicious prompts and therefore, the framework might not generalize well.

*   **Impact:** If the proposed framework proves robust in broader testing, it could have a considerable impact on the security of LLMs. It offers a practical, scalable, and adaptable solution to address the ongoing challenge of adversarial attacks. It lowers the bar for maintaining security, making LLMs more resilient in real-world deployments.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM security. The combination of prompt filtering and adversarial research summarization provides a compelling and practical solution for addressing a critical problem. While there are potential limitations, the benefits of the framework, particularly the ability to adapt dynamically without retraining, outweigh these concerns. The experimental results support the effectiveness of the approach. The work addresses scalability, adaptability, and interpretability, essential qualities for LLM defense mechanisms.
However, the paper could be strengthened by including the implementation details of the framework and by performing more tests on a wider range of LLMs and adversarial prompts.
Because of the identified strengths and the limitations that can be improved, the paper deserves a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Model See Model Do: Speech-Driven Facial Animation with Style Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01319v1)
- **Authors**: Yifang Pan, Karan Singh, Luiz Gustavo Hafemann
- **Abstract**: Speech-driven 3D facial animation plays a key role in applications such as virtual avatars, gaming, and digital content creation. While existing methods have made significant progress in achieving accurate lip synchronization and generating basic emotional expressions, they often struggle to capture and effectively transfer nuanced performance styles. We propose a novel example-based generation framework that conditions a latent diffusion model on a reference style clip to produce highly expressive and temporally coherent facial animations. To address the challenge of accurately adhering to the style reference, we introduce a novel conditioning mechanism called style basis, which extracts key poses from the reference and additively guides the diffusion generation process to fit the style without compromising lip synchronization quality. This approach enables the model to capture subtle stylistic cues while ensuring that the generated animations align closely with the input speech. Extensive qualitative, quantitative, and perceptual evaluations demonstrate the effectiveness of our method in faithfully reproducing the desired style while achieving superior lip synchronization across various speech scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Model See Model Do: Speech-Driven Facial Animation with Style Control" introduces a novel approach to generating stylistic 3D facial animations from speech. The system takes an audio sequence and a style reference video as input.  It uses a variational autoencoder (VAE) to extract style features from the reference video and a diffusion model conditioned on both audio and style features to produce the animation. A key innovation is the "style basis," a set of static poses derived from the style reference that guides the diffusion process, ensuring adherence to the reference style without compromising lip synchronization. The paper presents quantitative and qualitative evaluations, including user studies, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing methods in style transfer, lip synchronization, and upper-face dynamics. The authors also release their code and dataset curation pipeline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the combination of several elements, specifically the *style basis* conditioning mechanism within a diffusion model for speech-driven facial animation. While diffusion models and example-based approaches exist in this domain, the style basis is a specific contribution aimed at better controlling style transfer. The use of joint training for style encoder alongside a diffusion model is also significant as prior approaches did not jointly consider style encoding and main generation task, and treated all negative non-matching clips as equally dissimilar.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the realism and expressiveness of automatically generated facial animations. It addresses a key limitation of existing methods: the difficulty in transferring nuanced performance styles. The reported improvements in quantitative metrics, the compelling qualitative results in the supplementary video, and the positive user study results suggest the method is effective. The release of code and a data curation pipeline is a significant positive as it allows for reproducibility and builds on existing facial animation models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Technical Contribution:** The style basis conditioning is a well-motivated and technically sound approach. The experiments validate its effectiveness.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation, including quantitative metrics, qualitative examples, and user studies, addressing both objective and subjective aspects of animation quality.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  The availability of code and data is a major strength that encourages further research.
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:**  The paper tackles the practical problem of generating facial animations with specific styles, which is relevant to various applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the style basis improves style transfer, the system's effectiveness may be limited when the audio and style reference contain conflicting emotional cues (as acknowledged in the paper).
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The model's performance is tied to the quality and diversity of the training data. It would be valuable to see the model's performance on more varied datasets, especially those containing more dynamic facial performances or diverse demographic representations.
    *   **Incremental Nature:** While novel in its specific combination, it still builds upon existing diffusion models and facial animation techniques. The core technology is not completely groundbreaking, and the focus is on refinement and application in a challenging domain.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in speech-driven facial animation by:
    *   Motivating the use of style basis-like conditioning mechanisms for more effective style transfer.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating style transfer methods in this domain.
    *   Inspiring further work on disentangling style from content in facial animations.
    *   Providing a readily-accessible platform for experimentation and extension.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of speech-driven facial animation with a clearly articulated, well-implemented, and thoroughly evaluated technique. While not revolutionary, the specific approach of using the "style basis" for style conditioning, combined with the joint training of style encoder, addresses a real-world problem and offers notable improvements over existing methods. The inclusion of open-source data and code significantly enhances the value of the work and promotes future advancements in the field. Therefore, it earns a rating of 7. Further work can be done on integrating other characteristics, such as tone or duration, and implementing additional gestures to add to the model's functionality.
- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### FreeInsert: Disentangled Text-Guided Object Insertion in 3D Gaussian Scene without Spatial Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01322v1)
- **Authors**: Chenxi Li, Weijie Wang, Qiang Li, Bruno Lepri, Nicu Sebe, Weizhi Nie
- **Abstract**: Text-driven object insertion in 3D scenes is an emerging task that enables intuitive scene editing through natural language. However, existing 2D editing-based methods often rely on spatial priors such as 2D masks or 3D bounding boxes, and they struggle to ensure consistency of the inserted object. These limitations hinder flexibility and scalability in real-world applications. In this paper, we propose FreeInsert, a novel framework that leverages foundation models including MLLMs, LGMs, and diffusion models to disentangle object generation from spatial placement. This enables unsupervised and flexible object insertion in 3D scenes without spatial priors. FreeInsert starts with an MLLM-based parser that extracts structured semantics, including object types, spatial relationships, and attachment regions, from user instructions. These semantics guide both the reconstruction of the inserted object for 3D consistency and the learning of its degrees of freedom. We leverage the spatial reasoning capabilities of MLLMs to initialize object pose and scale. A hierarchical, spatially aware refinement stage further integrates spatial semantics and MLLM-inferred priors to enhance placement. Finally, the appearance of the object is improved using the inserted-object image to enhance visual fidelity. Experimental results demonstrate that FreeInsert achieves semantically coherent, spatially precise, and visually realistic 3D insertions without relying on spatial priors, offering a user-friendly and flexible editing experience.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FreeInsert: Disentangled Text-Guided Object Insertion in 3D Gaussian Scene without Spatial Priors":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FreeInsert, a novel framework for inserting objects into 3D Gaussian Splatting scenes using only text prompts (and optionally an image prompt) without requiring spatial priors like 2D masks or 3D bounding boxes. It achieves this by disentangling object generation from spatial placement through the use of foundation models: MLLMs (Multi-Modal Large Language Models), LGMs (Large Gaussian Models), and diffusion models. The framework comprises four stages: (1) An MLLM-based object insertion parser extracts structured semantics from the text prompt. (2) An initialization stage leverages large models for object generation and DoF (Degrees of Freedom) initialization. (3) Hierarchical Spatial Aware Refinement stage refines the object's placement using spatially-aware score distillation sampling (SSDS), guided by spatial semantics and priors inferred from the MLLM. (4) Object appearance refinement stage fine-tunes the object's visual quality by utilizing multi-view renderings. The experimental results demonstrate that FreeInsert enables semantically coherent, spatially precise, and visually realistic 3D insertions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The most significant novelty lies in the unsupervised approach to 3D object insertion, eliminating the need for manual spatial priors which are typically required in existing methods. The use of MLLMs to parse text instructions into structured semantics for guiding object generation and placement is a clever idea. The hierarchical spatial-aware refinement using SSDS coupled with MLLM-derived spatial priors for robust placement is also a valuable contribution. The combination of these elements into a complete pipeline is what constitutes the novelty. Prior methods tend to rely heavily on either manual input or struggle with spatial consistency. The disentanglement of object generation and placement is a sound architectural decision.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from enhancing the accessibility and ease of use of 3D scene editing. By removing the reliance on spatial priors, FreeInsert makes the 3D editing process more intuitive and scalable. The capability to handle diverse scenarios, including object-centric, human-centric, and complex outdoor scenes, demonstrates the practical applicability of the proposed framework. The improvement in object consistency and quality over existing methods (as shown in qualitative and quantitative evaluations) further validates its significance. The paper establishes a strong baseline for unsupervised 3D object insertion.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Unsupervised Approach:** Removing the reliance on spatial priors makes the system much more user-friendly and practical.
    *   **Effective Use of Foundation Models:** The integration of MLLMs, LGMs, and diffusion models for parsing, generation, and refinement is well-executed.
    *   **Hierarchical Refinement:** The hierarchical refinement stage effectively improves object placement by considering both global and local context.
    *   **Visual Quality:** The appearance refinement stage produces high-quality results that blend seamlessly with the existing scene.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough qualitative and quantitative comparisons with state-of-the-art methods, as well as ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of each component.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on MLLM Performance:** The performance of FreeInsert heavily relies on the accuracy and spatial understanding capabilities of the underlying MLLMs. Any limitations or biases present in the MLLMs could propagate to the insertion results.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The use of multiple foundation models, especially during the refinement stages, may result in high computational costs. This may limit the applicability of the method for real-time or interactive applications.
    *   **Generalization Limitations:** While the paper demonstrates results on diverse scenes, it is possible that the framework may struggle with more complex or unusual spatial arrangements that are not well-represented in the MLLM's training data.
    *   **Subjectivity:** As with any generative model, the evaluation metrics are partially subjective. The CLIP similarity score, while informative, does not fully capture the nuances of human perception.

*   **Potential Influence:** FreeInsert has the potential to influence future research in 3D scene editing by promoting unsupervised approaches that leverage foundation models. It could also inspire new methods for improving the spatial reasoning and manipulation capabilities of MLLMs. Furthermore, the disentangled architecture could be adopted or adapted for other generative tasks in 3D.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

FreeInsert demonstrates strong novelty and significance by providing a practical and user-friendly solution for 3D object insertion. The unsupervised approach addresses a major limitation of existing methods and enhances the accessibility of 3D scene editing. The well-designed framework and comprehensive evaluation further support its value. However, the dependency on MLLM performance and potential computational costs prevent it from achieving a higher score. The score reflects the significant contribution of the paper while acknowledging its limitations and potential for future improvement. The work presents a solid advancement in the field and provides a foundation for future research directions.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Provable Efficiency of Guidance in Diffusion Models for General Data Distribution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01382v1)
- **Authors**: Gen Li, Yuchen Jiao
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have emerged as a powerful framework for generative modeling, with guidance techniques playing a crucial role in enhancing sample quality. Despite their empirical success, a comprehensive theoretical understanding of the guidance effect remains limited. Existing studies only focus on case studies, where the distribution conditioned on each class is either isotropic Gaussian or supported on a one-dimensional interval with some extra conditions. How to analyze the guidance effect beyond these case studies remains an open question. Towards closing this gap, we make an attempt to analyze diffusion guidance under general data distributions. Rather than demonstrating uniform sample quality improvement, which does not hold in some distributions, we prove that guidance can improve the whole sample quality, in the sense that the average reciprocal of the classifier probability decreases with the existence of guidance. This aligns with the motivation of introducing guidance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the theoretical underpinnings of diffusion guidance, a technique used to enhance the quality of samples generated by diffusion models.  It addresses the gap in understanding how guidance works for general data distributions, moving beyond previous case studies that focused on specific distributions like isotropic Gaussians or distributions supported on one-dimensional intervals.  Instead of demonstrating a uniform improvement in sample quality (which the authors show *doesn't* always hold), the paper proves that guidance improves overall sample quality by decreasing the average reciprocal of the classifier probability. This metric is akin to the Inception Score (IS). The authors extend their analysis to more practical scenarios, including discrete-time implementations and the presence of score estimation errors. They also provide numerical validations of their theoretical results on Gaussian Mixture Models (GMMs) and the ImageNet dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its attempt to provide a more general theoretical analysis of diffusion guidance. Previous works have been limited to specific distribution classes, whereas this paper's analysis is applicable to a broader range of data distributions. The finding that guidance does *not* guarantee uniform improvement in sample quality, even for GMMs, is also a valuable observation. However, the core result – improving the average reciprocal of the classifier probability – while insightful, is arguably a limited type of performance guarantee. It would be more significant if the authors can extend the analysis and prove more general results, like sample diversity.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes to a better understanding of why guidance works. By demonstrating a theoretical improvement in a metric related to the Inception Score, the paper provides a degree of rigor lacking in previous empirical-focused research. Furthermore, the authors extend their analysis to discrete-time and imperfect score estimation settings, increasing the practicality of their results. However, the metric chosen for analysis, the expectation of the *reciprocal* of classifier probability, is less common and intuitive than, say, directly improving the classifier probability itself or its logarithm (as used in IS). Thus, the impact on practical applications may be limited.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses an important theoretical gap in the understanding of diffusion guidance.
    *   Provides a more general analysis than previous case-study approaches.
    *   The analysis is extended to practical implementation scenarios.
    *   Numerical experiments partially validate the theoretical results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The chosen metric (average reciprocal of classifier probability) is not as widely used or easily interpretable as other metrics (e.g., direct improvement of classifier probability).
    *   The practical implications of improving this specific metric might be limited.
    *   The analysis, while more general than previous work, could still be strengthened to provide sharper guarantees that are easier to connect to real-world applications.
    * The experimental results are limited. It may be beneficial to have more complete experiments to see if the finding can be expanded.

**Overall Justification:**

The paper takes a solid step in addressing the theoretical limitations surrounding diffusion guidance. It provides a more general analysis and highlights potential pitfalls (non-uniform improvement). However, the choice of metric and the limitations in connecting this metric to tangible application benefits prevent it from being considered a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 6/10

### Carbon Aware Transformers Through Joint Model-Hardware Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01386v1)
- **Authors**: Irene Wang, Newsha Ardalani, Mostafa Elhoushi, Daniel Jiang, Samuel Hsia, Ekin Sumbul, Divya Mahajan, Carole-Jean Wu, Bilge Acun
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of machine learning (ML) systems necessitates a more comprehensive evaluation of their environmental impact, particularly their carbon footprint, which comprises operational carbon from training and inference execution and embodied carbon from hardware manufacturing and its entire life-cycle. Despite the increasing importance of embodied emissions, there is a lack of tools and frameworks to holistically quantify and optimize the total carbon footprint of ML systems. To address this, we propose CATransformers, a carbon-aware architecture search framework that enables sustainability-driven co-optimization of ML models and hardware architectures. By incorporating both operational and embodied carbon metrics into early design space exploration of domain-specific hardware accelerators, CATransformers demonstrates that optimizing for carbon yields design choices distinct from those optimized solely for latency or energy efficiency. We apply our framework to multi-modal CLIP-based models, producing CarbonCLIP, a family of CLIP models achieving up to 17% reduction in total carbon emissions while maintaining accuracy and latency compared to state-of-the-art edge small CLIP baselines. This work underscores the need for holistic optimization methods to design high-performance, environmentally sustainable AI systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Carbon Aware Transformers Through Joint Model-Hardware Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CATransformers, a framework for co-optimizing ML models (specifically CLIP-based models) and hardware architectures to minimize the total carbon footprint, considering both operational and embodied carbon. CATransformers uses multi-objective Bayesian optimization to explore the design space, balancing accuracy, latency, energy, and carbon emissions. The framework includes an ML Model Evaluator that uses pruning and fine-tuning and a Hardware Estimator that leverages open-source profiling tools. The authors apply CATransformers to CLIP models, creating CarbonCLIP, which achieves significant carbon reductions (up to 17%) compared to baselines while maintaining accuracy and latency. The paper highlights the trade-offs between carbon, latency, and energy, and the importance of hardware-model co-optimization for sustainable AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the holistic approach to carbon-aware ML system design. While components like NAS and hardware accelerator optimization exist, CATransformers distinguishes itself by explicitly integrating embodied carbon alongside operational carbon and jointly optimizing across both model and hardware architectures. Addressing multi-modal models such as CLIP is a plus given their increasing usage and computational cost. The insight that traditional optimization metrics like latency and energy don't necessarily translate to minimal carbon emissions is valuable.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for sustainable AI. As ML models grow larger and more ubiquitous, their carbon footprint becomes a pressing concern. The tools and methodologies introduced by CATransformers offer a tangible path toward designing more environmentally responsible AI systems. The focus on edge inference is particularly relevant, as it addresses the growing deployment of ML in resource-constrained environments. The framework's capability to reveal hardware design trade-offs for different carbon optimization strategies could significantly impact future accelerator designs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper takes a well-rounded view of the carbon footprint, considering both embodied and operational emissions.
    *   **Practical Framework:** CATransformers provides a concrete, working framework that can be used to explore and optimize ML systems for carbon efficiency.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The CarbonCLIP results demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework in reducing carbon emissions while maintaining performance. The Pareto frontier analysis provides valuable insights into design trade-offs.
    *   **Clear Insights:** The paper articulates several key takeaways, such as the trade-offs between carbon and latency, the importance of tailoring hardware to model architectures, and the impact of compute constraints on optimization outcomes.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Scope:** While CLIP is a popular model, the paper's focus limits the generalizability of the findings. Expanding the framework to other model architectures and domains would strengthen the work.
    *   **Simplified Embodied Carbon Estimation:** The embodied carbon estimation, while using ACT, relies on simplifying assumptions about hardware manufacturing locations and grid intensities. More granular and context-aware embodied carbon models could enhance the accuracy of the results.
    *   **Edge-Inference Specificity**: The work is tailored towards edge inference systems, limiting the broader applicabilty towards other deployments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future ML systems and hardware accelerators. The framework could be adopted by researchers and engineers seeking to reduce the environmental impact of their work. The insights into carbon-aware optimization strategies could inform the development of new hardware and software tools. The co-optimization framework has the potential to be extended to broader range of models and deployment scenarios.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the individual components used in CATransformers are not entirely novel, the integration and application to the carbon-aware design of ML systems is compelling. The concrete methodology and empirical results demonstrate significant potential. However, the limitations in model scope and the simplified embodied carbon estimation prevent a higher score. Also, there is potential for improvement in quantifying the effects of each design parameters for hardware and the model architecture towards the final carbon emission.

Score: 7

### VIDSTAMP: A Temporally-Aware Watermark for Ownership and Integrity in Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01406v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammadreza Teymoorianfard, Shiqing Ma, Amir Houmansadr
- **Abstract**: The rapid rise of video diffusion models has enabled the generation of highly realistic and temporally coherent videos, raising critical concerns about content authenticity, provenance, and misuse. Existing watermarking approaches, whether passive, post-hoc, or adapted from image-based techniques, often struggle to withstand video-specific manipulations such as frame insertion, dropping, or reordering, and typically degrade visual quality. In this work, we introduce VIDSTAMP, a watermarking framework that embeds per-frame or per-segment messages directly into the latent space of temporally-aware video diffusion models. By fine-tuning the model's decoder through a two-stage pipeline, first on static image datasets to promote spatial message separation, and then on synthesized video sequences to restore temporal consistency, VIDSTAMP learns to embed high-capacity, flexible watermarks with minimal perceptual impact. Leveraging architectural components such as 3D convolutions and temporal attention, our method imposes no additional inference cost and offers better perceptual quality than prior methods, while maintaining comparable robustness against common distortions and tampering. VIDSTAMP embeds 768 bits per video (48 bits per frame) with a bit accuracy of 95.0%, achieves a log P-value of -166.65 (lower is better), and maintains a video quality score of 0.836, comparable to unwatermarked outputs (0.838) and surpassing prior methods in capacity-quality tradeoffs. Code: Code: \url{https://github.com/SPIN-UMass/VidStamp}
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the VidStamp paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VidStamp, a novel watermarking framework specifically designed for video diffusion models.  VidStamp embeds per-frame or per-segment messages directly into the latent space of temporally-aware video diffusion models. This is achieved by fine-tuning the decoder of the diffusion model in a two-stage process: first on static images to promote spatial message separability, and then on synthesized video to restore temporal consistency. The method aims to provide robust, high-capacity watermarks with minimal impact on video quality. It leverages the architecture of the video diffusion decoder, offering benefits like tamper localization and ownership verification without adding inference overhead. Experiments on Stable Video Diffusion (SVD) show that VidStamp achieves a good balance between watermark capacity, perceptual quality, and robustness against distortions and tampering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in adapting in-model watermarking techniques specifically for video diffusion models, addressing the temporal consistency issues that arise when simply extending image-based techniques. The two-stage fine-tuning process to ensure both spatial watermark separability and temporal consistency is also a significant contribution. The segment-wise embedding also introduces a good amount of flexibility in adapting watermarks depending on use case. Compared to Stable Signature for images, VidStamp is well adapted to video, and solves several problems of doing naive approaches for video.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it addresses a growing need for content authentication and provenance tracking in the era of AI-generated videos. Robust watermarking can play a crucial role in combating misinformation, impersonation, and tampering. The method's ability to localize temporal tampering is particularly valuable. While previous work addressed robustness against common corruptions, the paper clearly attempts to develop effective methods for robustness against adversarial attacks, though there's room for future improvements in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Temporally-aware approach:** The method effectively leverages the temporal components of video diffusion models for watermark embedding.
    *   **High Capacity:**  The paper demonstrates high watermarking capacity (768 bits per video) compared to baseline approaches.
    *   **Good Quality:** VidStamp maintains high video quality (comparable to unwatermarked outputs), minimizing perceptual impact.
    *   **Tamper Localization:** The method provides unique tamper localization capabilities, identifying the specific frames that have been altered.
    *   **No Inference Overhead:** Since watermarks are embedded during generation, there is no additional computational cost during inference.
    *   **Robustness:** Demonstrated comparable or superior robustness to various types of distortion and tampering compared to baseline models.
    *   **Careful Evaluation:** It uses a strong set of metrics, including Log P-value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Model Access Required:** The method requires access to the model's decoder for fine-tuning, limiting its applicability to scenarios with closed-source or black-box APIs. This reduces deployment flexibility.
    *   **Training Complexity:** The two-stage fine-tuning process adds complexity and computational cost to the training pipeline.
    *   **Limited Protection against Targeted Removal Attacks:** As acknowledged, the paper does not thoroughly address sophisticated adversarial attacks aimed at removing the watermark. While good performance is shown with standard distortions, an end to end threat model incorporating more adversarial attacks would significantly boost its standing.
    *   **Comparison Baseline selection:** While the baselines (VideoSeal, VideoShield, RivaGAN) are representative, they aren't necessarily the absolute SOTA on every dimension of video watermarking, but there aren't many options here given the relatively newer nature of video diffusion models.
    *   **Segmentation is not well defined:** The paper mentions the implementation of the segmentation phase, though it is missing critical definitions of segmentations themselves.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research on watermarking for generative models, particularly in the video domain. It provides a strong foundation for developing more robust and practical watermarking solutions. It also highlights the importance of considering temporal aspects and tamper localization in video watermarking. Future work may build upon VidStamp by addressing its limitations, such as improving resistance to adversarial attacks and exploring alternative embedding strategies.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of video watermarking. Its novel adaptation of in-model watermarking for video diffusion models, along with its demonstration of high capacity, good quality, and tamper localization capabilities, make it a valuable addition to the literature.  However, the practical limitations of requiring model access and the acknowledged vulnerability to targeted removal attacks prevent it from achieving a higher score. It also could use more rigorous details in its implementation of segmentations.

Score: 7.5
